19.383     ,     narratives  [                       id  2,             information     Options for Youth Victor Valley, Inc. Is an independent public charter school serving grades 7-12. We offer a blended learning program comprising independent study packets, an online curriculum, and small group instruction classes. We tailor the students' program to fit their needs. The mission of Options for Youth is to create an educational choice for all students. Our staff connects with students to empower and inspire them to achieve their goals and make their dreams a reality. Options for Youth will assist students as they develop supportive relationships that enable them to manage work life and familial responsibilities. Our vision is to re-engage students by giving them the skills and the confidence to become lifelong learners who contribute to their community.   ,             subSectionId  2,             cdsCode  36679343630670,             yearId  15,             userId  null,             lastUpdated  2024-01-15T14 07 23.557         ,                       id  3,             information     Options for Youth \u2013 Victor Valley regularly communicates with students and parents through various means. Families will be encouraged to sign up for Family Sensei (Edmentum) and Parent Portal (StudentTrac) to track their students' academic progress in real-time. Options for Youth Victor Valley has implemented a virtual hub website for students/parents where all information is posted, including student-led groups and open meeting times for counselors. Notice of school events will be posted on school websites. School Messenger will be used for mass communication purposes with mass emails, texts, and robocalls to facilitate effective communication and engagement with all stakeholders. School Messenger allows for translation in multiple languages, and bilingual staff is available for translation. Mailings in English and Spanish will also go out in paper versions to increase accessibility.Teachers communicate with parents throughout each school month through email, text, Zoom or Google Meets, and phone calls to discuss student academic performance and their progress towards graduation. Teachers use these monthly communications to collaborate with parents/guardians to ensure student progress. Parent conferences take place at least twice a year. These meetings are available in person or virtually to accommodate parents' schedules. Teachers share student academic progress and plan for the upcoming semester during these conferences. Additionally, parents can speak with the administration and staff through set appointments anytime during the year. Parents are invited to Back to School Nights, Open Houses, Wellness events as awards nights, events for our graduating seniors, and other events held throughout the year to celebrate student achievements. We offer various sporting events such as soccer, flag football, softball, basketball, bowling, and Esports. Events to promote social-emotional opportunities and prepare all students for college and career prep are offered in person and/or virtual throughout the year.Post-secondarily, Counselors are available to meet with all incoming new students, do monthly progress checks, meet all seniors, and are part of transitional planning with students with disabilities. Stakeholder engagement nights are also offered throughout the school year in person or virtually, including FAFSA workshops, resume building, and career chats. Reclassification events, college signing night, senior social, senior breakfast, and other school celebrations. Student ;Leadership meets regularly and facilitates school site events to increase student engagement and a sense of school community and connectedness. Parents are encouraged to attend our ;FAFSA Workshops and Senior Nights to ensure that parents know how to complete the FAFSA application line by line with our school academic counselors. The counselors also conduct senior meetings to develop a plan for each student to reach her/his goal of graduation and post-secondary plan.Moreover, parents are invited to our celebratory functions for students. These events include the following  a Senior Signing Day, which celebrates our students\u2019 accomplishments of getting accepted into a four-year university; a Bilingual Scholars Celebrations, which celebrates our English Language Learner students reclassification and academic progress; and a seasonal sports banquets, which acknowledges our student-athletes these will be held in person. ;Students and parents are also encouraged to meet with our Career Pathways Coordinators to help guide students to career interests, help with resume writing, and find internships and jobs.   ,             subSectionId  3,             cdsCode  36679343630670,             yearId  15,             userId  null,             lastUpdated  2024-01-15T14 26 17.623         ,                       id  8,             information     The school is always in compliance with all laws, rules, and regulations which is outlined by the School Site Safety Plan. The safety plan was updated and approved by the Board in January 2024. The elements of the plan include the following topics  Child Abuse Reporting, Disaster Procedures, Notification of Dangerous Individuals, Harassment Policy, Safe Ingress and Egress, Procedures of School Discipline, Bullying and Intimidation, School Dress Code, and Crime Assessment.The plan also outlines designated meeting places for individuals during a disaster, a ;procedure to release students to guardians or authorities, an assignment of specific ;responsibilities, emergency exit strategies, and a psychological/trauma care plan. Each ;school site has a site emergency liaison, who is responsible for ensuring the emergency kit is updated with current first aid supplies, coordinating the emergency plan, maintaining a list of community resources, and holding regular disaster drills. Fire, earthquake, active shooter, and lockdown drills are held each quarter throughout the school year. For the safety of the students, all visitors must sign in at the front desk and check in with the center coordinator before entering the school site. Furthermore, all students and staff must wear a visible Identification card at all times.Options for Youth Victor Valley has worked closely with the Public Health departments to develop an Exposure Management Plan in alignment with California State &amp; San Bernardino County Department of Health Offices and with guidance from the California Department of Education. Our top priority is the safety of our staff and students, and the Exposure Management plan was developed to ensure their safety. ;   ,             subSectionId  8,             cdsCode  36679343630670,             yearId  15,             userId  null,             lastUpdated  2024-01-15T14 29 04.99         ,                       id  10,             information     Preventive Maintenance was performed in Victorville 3 and Victorville 4  November 2022. Victorville 1  December 2023. Preventative maintenance was 100% completed for all centers. Centers are maintained in good condition through regular maintenance and ongoing repairs as needed through our internal work order portal in which our Facilities Technicians manage on a daily basis. Preventative Maintenance is performed on a monthly, quarterly, or as needed basis including on an annual basis. The overall facilities\u2019 ratings are based on the percentage of completion of the preventive maintenance and the condition of the facilities. The facility technicians for these centers will continue to manage all requests or repairs in a timely manner. Otherwise, the centers are in GOOD condition.   ,             subSectionId  10,             cdsCode  36679343630670,             yearId  15,             userId  null,             lastUpdated  2024-01-15T14 12 56.34         ,                       id  18,             information     Options for Youth \u2013 Victor Valley is an independent study program serving students from grades 7th through 12th with blended learning opportunities. In addition to students working independently on their coursework, they have the opportunity to attend small group instruction classes for additional support in core courses such as math, English, and science. Students are also given the opportunity to take rigorous online courses through our Edmentum curriculum.The school also offers small-group Junior High classes. Additionally, students can take advantage of our on-site math and English tutoring to support them with their homework assignments and to help support students through their independent study work.For each course, students are provided all materials necessary to be fully engaged in the coursework, including textbooks, student activity workbooks, materials and supplies, access to technology, and educational software. To ensure academic success, our teachers monitor student progress daily by providing feedback, administering assessments, ensuring appropriate class placement, and meeting with students and parents regularly to discuss their academic performance.Students with Individual Educational Plans are provided with an instructional team composed of a General Education teacher, Special Education Specialist, School Psychologist, and paraprofessional to ensure academic support is in place for the students to access the curriculum and meet their goals. Students also have access to math and English tutors.OFY- VV has full-time Post-Secondary School Counselors, and we also offer Career Pathway Coordinators at each site this allows students access to support in post-secondary planning and career explorations. The counselors support seniors through community service opportunities, events, field trips, college tours, career fairs, ASVAB testing, help with SAT/ACT registration, study materials, and financial aid workshops. OFY-Victor Valley provides a plethora of student services geared towards enhancing the student\u2019s educational and social-emotional experience through field trips, domestic and international trips, student council, sports programs, community service activities, grad night, prom, school dances, tutoring, senior socials, career fairs, EL celebrations, a senior signing event, Wellness fairs SEL curriculum, school site SEL workshops, and various celebratory banquets. We expose our students to experiential learning opportunities, providing social-emotional experiences and support. OFY-Victor Valley offers CTE programs to prepare students for the workforce and post-secondary opportunities. Throughout the school year, the students are exposed to different career paths and opportunities to network with businesses and career fields. We work with our local community college to offer dual enrollment opportunities. We also work with local businesses to obtain internships for our students, along with scholarships for college.OFY-VV continually invests in the development of the instructional program by offering ;various professional development opportunities to its staff. One of our priorities is to ;continue strengthening our program by providing school-sponsored and outside ;opportunities for staff to increase their effectiveness and engagement of students by using proven instructional strategies, making data-driven decisions, and standards-aligned instruction. To create cohesive instruction and increase collaboration, staff participates in department-specific professional learning communities, school site meetings with school administration, and cross-department workshops with Special Education, Mental Health Department, and English Learner Specialists throughout the year. In addition, we continue utilizing the Renaissance STAR program to assess and monitor students in Reading and Mathematics. This assessment tool provides instructional staff with a needs assessment for each student. This enables teachers to create instructional plans accordingly for each student.Students who need additional support in Reading or Mathematics are also provided with ;instructional intervention through Achieve 3000, Exact Path Math, and Reading programs. The I Lit Program is also used to offer additional support to our English Learner students. ;The school has increased its online course offerings for its students by growing its Edmentum online courses. The School Psychologists provides monthly awareness events and information to students on various topics regarding mental health and wellness. Topics include Anti-Bullying, Mental Health, Suicide Prevention, Human Trafficking, Teen Dating, and Violence Awareness. In addition, we provide students with healthy food options in accordance with our Wellness Program guidelines, offer school spirit wear to promote the school community and Transportation assistance.   ,             subSectionId  18,             cdsCode  36679343630670,             yearId  15,             userId  null,             lastUpdated  2024-01-15T14 38 30.143         ,                       id  35,             information     Students in the charter high school have had access to CTE programs in the industry sectors of Health Science and Medical Technology, Building and Construction Trades, Agriculture and Natural Resources, and Information Technology. These programs provide practical training in high-demand, sustainable careers using industry technologies. Hands-on skill building is supplemented with career and academic content and theory for each pathway. Students in these programs can earn industry-recognized certifications, licensure, or designations and become eligible for employment within industry-specific organizations.The first program available to students is the Health Science and Medical Technology  Nursing pathway. In this program, students develop nursing skills using hands-on equipment and technology. They gain real-world skills by working with medical technologies, hearing from guest speakers, and studying nursing practices and methodologies. The curriculum covers human anatomy, licensure and responsibilities in the nursing industry, and hands-on components of the CNA exam. Additionally, students have the opportunity to earn CPR/First Aid/AED certification. Practice and content knowledge culminate in CNA exam preparation. Courses include Introduction to Nursing Sem A, Introduction to Nursing Sem B, Certified Nurse Aide Sem A, and Certified Nurse Aide Sem B.The second program is the Building and Construction Trades pathway, focusing on building construction skills through training with hand and power tools on increasingly advanced carpentry projects. The program includes the opportunity to earn an OSHA-10 safety certification. The pathway involves visits from industry representatives and career exploration within the building and construction trades. Students learn about construction materials, safety, hand tools, power tools, construction techniques, blueprint reading, and more. CTE state standards and objectives align with the United Brotherhood of Carpenters\u2019 pre-apprenticeship curriculum, supported by the local union council, the Southwest Council of Carpenters. Students can earn union training certificates by completing their CTE coursework. Courses include Construction 1A, Construction 1B, Construction 2A, and Construction 2B.The third program falls within the Agricultural and Natural Resources Pathways, culminating in the opportunity to earn the designation Veterinary Assistant. In this program, students study animal science and veterinary medicine, learning about the care, diagnosis, and treatment of both small and large animals, as well as parasitology, toxicology, veterinary tools, and more. Students combine knowledge acquisition through coursework with building veterinary skills using industry tools and equipment. The program offers the opportunity to complete a NAVTA approved program, including theory and an externship, allowing students to take the Veterinary Assistant examination upon completion. Upon passage, students can earn their Veterinary Assistant Designation. Coursework includes Intro to Animal Science Sem A and Sem B, and Intro to Veterinary Science Sem A and Sem B.The fourth program gives students access to Information Technology. In this program, students build knowledge relevant to the information technology sector, learning about hardware, software, the history of computing, the internet of all things, and more. They develop skills in various office suite applications such as word processing, spreadsheets, and presentation software. Students engage in hands-on training, including building a desktop computer, and use industry technology to practice troubleshooting computer issues and other tasks through simulations. The pathway culminates with the CompTIA ITF+ Exam Prep capstone, preparing students to earn this industry certification. Coursework includes Principles of Information Technology Sem A and B, and CompTIA ITF+ with Exam Prep Sem 1 and Sem 2.CTE programs are offered to students as five credit elective courses. The charter school offers these programs to students with an interest in entry level skills development in these trade professions. Additionally, the school supports career development by incorporating soft career skills such as interviewing skills and resume writing workshops. Safety equipment and PPE is provided to every student, as well as coaching and moral support when applying to employers during the hiring process. Outcomes are measured by CTE instructors weekly evaluating student attendance, hands-on skills participation, assignment completion, and certification completion progress. The CTE program is influenced by a Business Advisory Committee including representatives from the Construction, IT, and Healthcare industries. The primary representative is Skyrocket\u2019s Chief Student Experience Officer.   ,             subSectionId  35,             cdsCode  36679343630670,             yearId  15,             userId  null,             lastUpdated  2024-01-15T22 40 40.837              ],     sarcSubSections  [                       sectionId  4,             title  CAASPP Test Results - English Language Arts (ELA) Grade 3,             id  47,             legalRequirements  EC Section 33126 (b)(1)(A) Public Law 107-110 Section 1111 (h)(2)(B) Public Law 107-110 Section 1111 (h)(1)(C),             definitions  For the SARC reporting year testing period, the percent of students by student group, total enrollment, number tested, percent tested, and achievement levels in CAASPP ELA, grades three through eight, and eleven. In the 2014 ;15 school year, the CAASPP consists of several key components, including   CAA, the 2014 ;15 CAA field test includes both ELA/literacy and mathematics in grades three through eight and eleven. The CAA is given to those students with significant cognitive disabilities whose disabilities prevent them from taking the Smarter Balanced Assessments with universal tools, designated supports, or accommodations. There are no 2014 ;15 CAA assessment results for the 2014 ;15 school year. Smarter Balanced, the 2014 ;15 Smarter Balanced Assessments include ELA/literacy in grades three through eight and grade eleven. Smarter Balanced Assessments are designed to measure student progress toward college and career readiness.  The assessments under the CAASPP show how well students are doing in relation to the state-adopted content standards. On each of these assessments, student aggregate scores are reported as achievement levels. Detailed information regarding the 2015 CAASPP results for each grade and achievement level can be found on the CDE CAASPP Results Web page at http //caaspp.cde.ca.gov/. Note  Data shared in the table may differ from the Adequate Yearly Progress (AYP) data because of the difference in inclusion and exclusion requirements. Scores are not shown when the number of students tested is ten or less, either because the number of students in this category is too small for statistical accuracy or to protect student privacy. In the 2014 ;15 school year, the CAASPP consists of several key components, including   CAA, the 2014 ;15 CAA field test includes both ELA/literacy and mathematics in grades three through eight and eleven. The CAA is given to those students with significant cognitive disabilities whose disabilities prevent them from taking the Smarter Balanced Assessments with universal tools, designated supports, or accommodations. There are no 2014 ;15 CAA assessment results for the 2014 ;15 school year. Smarter Balanced, the 2014 ;15 Smarter Balanced Assessments include ELA/literacy in grades three through eight and grade eleven. Smarter Balanced Assessments are designed to measure student progress toward college and career readiness.  The assessments under the CAASPP show how well students are doing in relation to the state-adopted content standards. On each of these assessments, student aggregate scores are reported as achievement levels. The formula for the percent of core academic classes taught and not taught by HQT at each level is  (The number of core academic classes taught/not taught by HQT)  ; divided by  ; (The total number of core academic classes) High-poverty schools are defined as those schools with student eligibility of approximately 40 percent or more in the FRPM program. Low-poverty schools are those with student eligibility of approximately 39 percent or less in the FRPM program. ESEA defines core academic subject areas as English, reading or language arts, math, science, foreign languages, civics and government, economics, arts, history and geography. However, the United States (U.S.) Department of Education encouraged states to consider their current teacher certification and student achievement standards to determine whether to allow broad categories or require subject-specific certification in the discrete fields of science and/or social science. Additionally, the U.S. Department of Education has given states the authority to determine its own definition of \the arts\. Chart 2 below portrays how the federal definition of &ldquo;core academic subjects&rdquo; has been aligned to correlate with California&rsquo;s certification and student achievement standards. Chart 2  ;Core Academic Subjects  ; Federal   English Reading/Language Arts Mathematics Science History Economics Civics/Government Geography Foreign Languages Arts  California  English/Language Arts and Reading (Includes reading intervention and CAHSEE-English classes) Mathematics (Includes math intervention and CAHSEE-Math classes) Biological Sciences Chemistry Geosciences Physics Social Science (Includes  History, Government, Economics, and Geography) Foreign Languages (specific) Drama/Theater (English Credential) Visual Arts Music Dance (Physical Education Credential)  Please note  Career Technical Education (CTE) courses that have been designated as an alternative pathway to graduation by the local governing board of the LEA per EC, Section 51225.3(b) are also designated as core academic subjects. By 2005 ;06 ESEA required that all teachers teaching in core academic subjects be \highly qualified.\ In general, ESEA requires that to be designated as highly qualified, a teacher must meet the following three criteria   Possession of a bachelor's degree; Possession of an appropriate California teaching credential; and Demonstrated core academic subject area competence by means of exam, coursework, advanced certification, or completion of the California High Objective Uniform State Standard of Evaluation (HOUSSE) in the subject area being taught. The assessment of estimated expenditures per pupil shall be reported in total, shall be reported in subtotal by restricted and unrestricted source, and shall include a reporting of the average of actual salaries paid to certificated instructional personnel at that school site. Restricted source means money whose use is restricted by legal requirement or by the donor. Examples include, but are not limited to, instructional materials, economic impact aid, and teacher and principal training funds. Unrestricted source means money that can be used for any lawful purpose, whose use is not restricted by legal requirement or by the donor. Examples include, but are not limited to, class size reduction, state lottery (not the instructional materials portion), and undesignated local parcel tax funds. For more information about classifying revenues and expenditures, see Procedure 310 in the California School Accounting Manual on the CDE Definitions, Instructions, &amp; Procedures web page at https //www.cde.ca.gov/fg/ac/sa/. LEAs should use a consistent methodology for calculating estimated per pupil expenditures at the school site and LEA levels. For pupil counts, LEAs should use the annual Average Daily Attendance (ADA) figure for the school site. The CDE calculates state average expenditures per pupil from an unrestricted source, using 2018 ;2019 data, for report cards in the ;2019 ;2020 school year. For pupil counts, the CDE uses the statewide ADA from the annual reporting period. The CDE&rsquo;s calculation is based on EC Section 41372 definitions (see the CDE Current Expense of Education &amp; Per-pupil Spending web page at https //www.cde.ca.gov/ds/fd/ec/ ;, modified to include only unrestricted sources in the calculation. In calculating the average actual teacher salary at each school site, LEAs should use a consistent methodology. Teachers include all certificated instructional personnel measured on a FTE basis. To calculate the average teacher salary at the school site, LEAs should divide the total actual salaries paid to certificated instructional personnel at the school site by the FTE of certificated instructional personnel at the school site. The CDE calculates LEA and state average teacher salaries, using 2018 ;2019 data, for report cards in the ;2019 ;2020 school year. Average teacher salaries at the LEA and state levels are derived from information collected on CDE Form J-90. These averages, which reflect only those salaries in school LEAs that submitted Form J-90, are calculated by dividing the salaries paid on the certificated salary schedule by the total number of FTE employees included on that schedule. The statewide and similar schools API ranks range from 1 to 10 and are established by school type (e.g., elementary, middle and high). A statewide rank of 1 for an elementary school means that the school has an API score in the lowest ten percent of all elementary schools in the state, while a statewide rank of 10 means that the school has an API score in the highest ten percent of all elementary schools in the state. The similar schools API rank reflects how a school compares to 100 schools, of the same type, with similar levels of opportunities and challenges based on 22 characteristics. A similar schools rank of 1 means that the school's academic performance is in the lowest ten percent of the 100 similar schools, while a similar schools rank of 10 means that the school's academic performance is in the top 10 percent of the 100 other similar schools. EC Section 52056(a) requiring API ranking of schools was repealed, effective July 1, 2013. As a result, beginning in 2014 and beyond, statewide and similar school rankings will no longer be produced. The 2015\u201316 SARC Report and subsequent SARC Reports will be amended to comply with current state requirements.  ,             dataSources  Data are provided by the CDE. Total number of pupils participating in CTE - ;are the non-duplicated counts as a result of pupils participating in more than one CTE program. Other data available on outcomes of the school's CTE programs, including data from related programs, such as the Workforce Investment Act, may also be provided. CTE Sequenced Courses - two or more courses that are intended to be taken in consecutive order to fulfill a program requirement. CTE Articulated Courses - courses that are part of an agreement between the high school and institutions of postsecondary education that aligns curriculum and instruction to provide a seamless transition from high school to the institution of postsecondary education in a career pathway. Number of school days in each of the most recent three school years (2018 ;2019, 2019 ;2020, and 2020 ;2021), that were dedicated to teachers and staff in the development and continuous professional growth to help them improve instruction. Teachers and staff include   All classroom teachers; New teachers (e.g., Beginning Teacher Support and Assessment [BTSA]); Non-classroom teachers; National Board Certified Teachers; Teachers experiencing difficulty/in need of improvement (e.g., Peer Assistance and Review); Paraprofessionals (e.g., instructional aides, teacher assistants); and Non-instructional support staff (e.g., clerical, custodial).  Provide the number of school days for each year (regardless of being full-days or partial-days). In the comments section of the electronic template or as a notation below the table in a vendor-created or custom-created template, the SARC coordinator may clarify for each year how many of these days were full-days and how many of these days were partial-days. The point of this section is to provide the reader with an understanding of how much time was dedicated towards staff development and continuous improvement for each of the school years. For the purposes of the SARC, a partial-day is defined as less than a full school day. Each school will determine what is a full school day based on its schedule. For purposes of the SARC, a school day is defined as a day upon which the school is in session, including non-holiday weekdays, within the period of time beginning on July 1 of one calendar year and ending on June 30 of the following calendar year, including during summer school session. Below are examples of how to calculate the number of school days dedicated to staff development and continuous improvement. Keep in mind that the number of school days is based on the number of days impacted by the Professional Development courses/meetings, not the number of teachers and staff attending, or the grade-level of the teachers, or the cumulative hours of the various teachers and staff attending. Also, it is important that the methodology of calculation should be consistent year over year in each school&rsquo;s SARC.  ; Example 1  The school has a 2-hour Professional Development course/meeting for teachers to attend. This course is scheduled during multiple 2-hour intervals during the same school day, and the various grade-level teachers attend at different times while substitutes tend to their classes during their absence. In the table, count this 1 Professional Development course/meeting as 1 school day and in the comments section or notation below the table this may benoted as &ldquo;1 partial day&rdquo;.  ; Example 2  The school has a 2-hour Professional Development course/meeting for teachers and staff to attend. This course is scheduled during multiple 2-hour intervals during three separate school days, and the various grade-level teachers and staff attend at different times and on different days. In the table, count this 1 Professional Development course/meeting as 3 school days and in the comments section or notation below the table this may be noted as &ldquo;3 partial days&rdquo;.  ; Example 3  The school has a 3-day (full school days) Professional Development course/meeting for teachers and staff to attend. This course is scheduled on nine different school days during the fall, and the various grade-level teachers and staff attend during different three days. In the table, count this 3-day Professional Development course/meeting as 9 school days and in the comments section or notation below the table this may be noted as &ldquo;9 full days&rdquo;.,             url  /Sections/ProfessionalNew,             description  null,             printUrl  /Sarc/ProfessionalNew,             sortOrder  10,             isNotRequired  false,             yearId  1         ,                       sectionId  6,             title  Student Support Services Staff ,             id  82,             legalRequirements  EC Section 33126 (b)(7),             definitions  For the SARC reporting year (2019 ;2020), the number of FTE counselors (academic, social/behavioral, or career development), and other student support services personnel who are assigned to the school.,             dataSources  Data are provided by the CDE. The number of FTE staff assigned to the school is a count of FTEs by support services classification. A full-time support services staff employee equals one FTE or 1.0; two support services staff employees reported to work different FTEs can add up to a percentage of an FTE. ;   Example 1  Support services staff member 1 works 0.5 FTE as a psychologist, and support services staff member 2 works 0.5 FTE as a psychologist. This aggregates up as a single 1.0 FTE in the psychologist classification.   Example 2  Support services staff member 1 works 0.5 FTE as a psychologist, and support services staff member 2 works 0.3 FTE as a psychologist, and support services staff member 3 works 1.0 FTE as a psychologist. This aggregates up as 1.8 FTE in the psychologist classification at this school. For any data cells with N/T values indicate that this school did not test students using the CAASPP for Science. The CDE developed a new California Science Test (CAST) based on the Next Generation Science Standards for California Public Schools (CA NGSS). The new CAST is aligned with these standards. ;CAST is required for all students in grades five and eight and once in high school (i.e., grade ten, eleven, or twelve) unless their individual education plan (IEP) indicates assessment with an alternate assessment. The CAA for Science is aligned with Science Core Content Connectors that are derived from the CA NGSS. The CAA for Science is required for students with the most significant cognitive disabilities who are unable to take the CAST even with accessibility supports and whose IEP indicates assessment with an alternate test. The CAA for Science is administered to eligible students in grades five and eight and once in high school (i.e., grade ten, eleven, or twelve). The 2020 ;2021 Science test results include the CAST only. Due to the pandemic, the CAA for Science was not operational during the 2020 ;2021 school year and will not be included in this report. Inclusion and exclusion rules are different for the SARC than for other public assessment reports. Scores are not shown when the number of students tested is ten or less, either because the number of students in this category is too small for statistical accuracy or to protect student privacy. Information about the CAASPP for Science can be found at the CDE California Science Test web page at https //www.cde.ca.gov/ta/tg/ca/caasppscience.asp,             url  /Sections/ScienceTestResults,             description  null,             printUrl  /Sarc/ScienceTestResults,             sortOrder  17,             isNotRequired  false,             yearId  1         ,                       sectionId  3,             title  Teacher Preparation and Placement,             id  87,             legalRequirements  EC Section 33126 (b)(5)Elementary and Secondary Education Act ;(ESEA) Section 1111 (h)(1)(C)(ix)ESEA Section 1111 (h)(5)(D),             definitions  For the 2019 ;2020 school year, at the school, district, and state levels, the number and percent of teacher authorization/assignment that are  &bull; Fully (Preliminary or Clear) Credentialed for Subject and Student Placement (properly assigned);&bull; Intern Credential Holders Properly Assigned; &bull; Teachers Without Credentials and Misassignments (&ldquo;ineffective&rdquo; under Every Student Succeeds Act [ESSA]);&bull; Credentialed Teachers Assigned Out-of-Field (&ldquo;out-of-field&rdquo; under ESSA); and &bull; Unknown For the 2019 ;2020 school year, at the school, district, and state levels, the total number and percent of teaching positions. Fully (Preliminary or Clear) Credentialed for Subject and Student Placement (properly assigned)  The teacher possesses a California preliminary or clear credential issued by the California Commission on Teacher Credentialing which permits them to teach the subject and grade levels that they are assigned. Intern Credential Holders Properly Assigned  The teacher possess a California intern credential issued by the California Commission on Teacher Credentialing which permits them to teach the subject and grade levels that they are assigned. Teachers Without Credentials and Misassignments (&ldquo;ineffective&rdquo; under ESSA)  An ineffective teacher is any of the following   An individual whose assignment is legally authorized by an emergency permit that does not require possession of a full teaching license; or A teacher who holds a teaching credential but does not possess a permit or authorization that temporarily allows them to teach outside of their credentialed area (misassigned); or An individual who holds no credential, permit, or authorization to teach in California.  &ldquo;Ineffective&rdquo; includes the following limited term emergency permits   Provisional Internship Permits; Short-Term Staff Permits; Variable Term Waivers; and Substitute permits or Teaching Permits for Statutory Leave (TSPL) holders serving as the teacher of record.  Credentialed Teachers Assigned Out-of-Field (&ldquo;out-of-field&rdquo; under ESSA)  A credentialed teacher who has not yet demonstrated subject matter competence in the subject area(s) or for the student population to which he or she is assigned. Under this definition, the following limited permits will be considered out of field   General Education Limited Assignment Permit (GELAP) Special Education Limited Assignment Permit (SELAP) Short-Term Waivers Emergency English Learner or Bilingual Authorization Permits Local Assignment Options  Unknown  These are instances where either information about the course assignment or the teacher is either missing or has been identified as incorrect. Therefore, an accurate assignment status cannot be currently made.,             dataSources  Data are provided by the CDE.The data source is the teacher data based on both the FTE submitted and certified by LEAs and/or charter schools as part of the annual CALPADS Fall 2 submission as well as the assignment monitoring outcome data determined during the annual assignment monitoring process conducted by the CTC through the California State Assignment Accountability System (CalSAAS).For information on the CalSAAS, visit the CALPADS Update Flash #201 web page at ;https //www.cde.ca.gov/ds/sp/cl/calpadsupdflash201.asp.For more information on the definitions listed above, refer to the Updated Teacher Equity Definitions web page at https //www.cde.ca.gov/pd/ee/teacherequitydefinitions.asp.An overview of the Teaching Assignment Monitoring Outcome (AMO) data used for the teacher tables in the SARC, including data sources, DataQuest reports, additional downloadable files, definitions, and a description of the methodology for processing the data, are available on the Information about the Teaching AMO Report web page at ;https //www.cde.ca.gov/ds/ad/tamoinfo.asp?For questions concerning the assignment of teachers outside their subject area of competence or the credential status of teachers, visit the California Commission on Teacher Credentialing website at https //www.ctc.ca.gov/. These data are provided by the LEA.,             url  /Sections/TeacherPlacement,             description  null,             printUrl  null,             sortOrder  2,             isNotRequired  true,             yearId  1         ,                       sectionId  3,             title  Teachers Without Credentials and Misassignments,             id  88,             legalRequirements  EC Section 33126 (b)(5)EC Section 33126 (b)(5)(A)EC Section 33126 (b)(5)(B),             definitions  For the 2019 ;2020 school year, at the school level, the percent of authorization/assignment of teachers   Permits/waivers; Misassignments; and Vacant Positions  For the 2019 ;2020 school year, at the school level, the total number of teachers without credentials and misassignments. The data in this table is based on Full Time Equivalent (FTE) status. One FTE equals one staff member working full time; one FTE could also represent two staff members who each work 50 percent of full time. Additionally, an assignment is defined as a position that an educator is assigned to based on setting, subject, and grade level. An authorization is defined as the services that an educator is authorized to provide to students. Permits/wavers  Teachers that are issued limited emergency permits by the California Commission on Teacher Credentialing that authorize them to temporarily teach courses and student groups they are assigned to without possessing the appropriate credential. These include  Provisional Internship Permits, Short-term Staff Permits, Variable Term Waivers as well as substitute permits or Teaching Permits for Statutory Leave. Under California&rsquo;s ESSA definition, these teachers are considered &ldquo;ineffective&rdquo;. Teacher Misassignments  The assignment of employees to teaching or pupil services positions for which the employee does not hold the required certificate, credential, or other statutory authorization. These services areas, for which the California Commission on Teacher Credentialing issues credentials, include Administrative Services, Student Personnel Services, Clinical/Rehabilitative Services, Library Media Services, and School Nurse or Other Health Services. Vacant Teacher Positions  The total number of the school&rsquo;s vacant teacher positions (the number of positions to which a single-designated certificated employee has not been assigned at the beginning of the year for an entire year or, if the position is for a one-semester course, a position of which a single-designated certificated employee has not been assigned at the beginning of a semester for an entire semester). Beginning of the year or semester means the first day classes necessary to serve all the students enrolled are established with a single-designated certificated employee assigned for the duration of the class, but not later than 20 working days after the first day students attend classes for that semester.,             dataSources  Data are provided by the CDE. The data source is the California State Assignment Accountability System (CalSAAS) uploaded by the Commission on Teacher Credentialing. For information on the CalSAAS, visit the CALPADS Update Flash #201 web page at https //www.cde.ca.gov/ds/sp/cl/calpadsupdflash201.asp. For more information on the definitions listed above, refer to the Updated Teacher Equity Definitions web page at https //www.cde.ca.gov/pd/ee/teacherequitydefinitions.asp. Socioeconomically Disadvantaged (SED) students are defined as students  (1) who are eligible for the FRPM program (also known as the National School Lunch Program, or NSLP), or have a direct certification for FRPMs, or (2) who are migrant, homeless, or foster youth, or (3) where neither of the parents were a high school graduate.  ;,             dataSources  Data are provided by the LEA. LEAs/schools will populate this table for schools in cases where the school administered a local assessment for ELA.  LEAs/schools will populate this table with double dashes (--) when the number of students is ten or less, either because the number of students in this category is too small for statistical accuracy or to protect student privacy. In cases where the school administered the CAASPP assessment, LEAs/schools will populate this table with &ldquo;N/A&rdquo; values in all cells, meaning this table is Not Applicable for this school. Instruction  If using a vendor-created or custom-created template, make duplicates of this table, as needed, in order to report all local assessments in ELA administered for the various grade spans (grades 3 through 8 and grade 11). If using the electronic SARC template, the system will provide the option for multiple local assessment tables and the selection of grade spans. For the SARC Reporting in the 2020 ;2021 School Year Only  Where the most viable option, LEAs were required to administer the statewide summative assessment in ELA and mathematics. Where a statewide summative assessment was not the most viable option for the LEA (or for one or more grade-level[s] within the LEA) due to the pandemic, LEAs were allowed to report results from a different assessment that met the criteria established by the State Board of Education (SBE) on March 16, 2021 (https //www.cde.ca.gov/be/ag/ag/yr21/documents/mar21item02addendum.docx). The assessments were required to be   Aligned with CA CCSS for ELA; Available to students in grades 3 through 8, and grade 11; and Uniformly administered across a grade, grade span, school, or district to all eligible students.  Options  Note that the CAAs could only be administered in-person following health and safety requirements. If it was not viable for the LEA to administer the CAAs in person with health and safety guidelines in place, the LEA was directed to not administer the tests. There were no other assessment options available for the CAAs. Where a statewide summative assessment was not the most viable option for the LEA (or for one or more grade-level[s] within the LEA) due to the pandemic, LEAs were allowed to report results from a different assessment that met the criteria established by the State Board of Education (SBE) on March 16, 2021 (https //www.cde.ca.gov/be/ag/ag/yr21/documents/mar21item02addendum.docx). The assessments were required to be   Aligned with CA CCSS for mathematics; Available to students in grades 3 through 8, and grade 11; and Uniformly administered across a grade, grade span, school, or district to all eligible students.  Options  Note that the CAAs could only be administered in-person following health and safety requirements. If it was not viable for the LEA to administer the CAAs in person with health and safety guidelines in place, the LEA was directed to not administer the tests. There were no other assessment options available for the CAAs. Schools administered the Smarter Balanced Summative Assessments for mathematics, other assessments that meet the SBE criteria, or a combination of both, and they could only choose one of the following   Smarter Balanced mathematics summative assessments; Other assessments meeting the SBE criteria; or Combination of Smarter Balanced mathematics summative assessments and other assessments as long as they were uniformly across a grade level. ,             url  /Sections/CaaSppLocal?gradeId=13&subject=Mathematics1,             description  null,             printUrl  null,             sortOrder  9,             isNotRequired  false,             yearId  13         ,                       sectionId  4,             title  Additional table if needed (table 2) - Local Assessment Test Results in Mathematics by Student Group,             id  100,             legalRequirements  EC Section 33126 (b)(1)(A) ESEA Section 1111 (h)(1)(C),             definitions  For the SARC reporting year (2020 ;2021) testing period, the total enrollment, number tested, percent tested, percent not tested, and percent at or above grade-level in Local Assessments of mathematics by student group, grades three through eight and grade eleven.
